Commutator subgroups and crystallographic quotients of virtual extensions of symmetric groups
Pravin Kumar, Tushar Kanta Naik, Neha Nanda, Mahender Singh

Abstract
. The virtual braid group V ​ B n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038} , the virtual twin group V ​ T n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038} and the virtual triplet group V ​ L n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038} are extensions of the symmetric group S n \mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038} , which are motivated by the Alexander-Markov correspondence for virtual knot theories. The kernels of natural epimorphisms of these groups onto the symmetric group S n \mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038} are the pure virtual braid group V ​ P n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29008_{\mathchar 29038} , the pure virtual twin group P ​ V ​ T n \mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038} and the pure virtual triplet group P ​ V ​ L n \mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038} , respectively. In this paper, we investigate commutator subgroups, pure subgroups and crystallographic quotients of these groups. We derive explicit finite presentations of the pure virtual triplet group P ​ V ​ L n \mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038} , the commutator subgroup V T n ′ \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}} of V ​ T n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038} and the commutator subgroup V L n ′ \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}} of V ​ L n \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038} . Our results complete the understanding of these groups, except that of V B n ′ \mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}} , for which the existence of a finite presentations is not known for n ≥ 4 \mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724 . We also prove that V L n / P V L n ′ \mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}} is a crystallographic group and give an explicit construction of infinitely many torsion elements in it.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 20F55, 20H15; Secondary 20F36

1.
Introduction

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, the symmetric group
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
admits a Coxeter presentation with generators
\{\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\}
and defining relations:

(1)

\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
for
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
,

(2)

\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29034}
for
\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29034\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
,

(3)

\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}
for
\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12906\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
.

By omitting all the relations of type (1), (2) or (3) one at a time from the preceding presentation, we obtain three canonical extensions of
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
, namely, the braid group
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
, the twin group
\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
and the triplet group
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
.

(1.0.3)

\displaystyle\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12349\Bigg\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}~\mathchar 12906~\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\hskip 8.50012pt\textrm{for}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\hskip 8.50012pt\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29034}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for}\hskip 8.50012pt\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29034\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\Bigg\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 314

The terms twin groups and triplet groups first appeared in the work
[
20
]
of Khovanov on
\mathchar 29003\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28953\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
subspace arrangements. The kernels of natural epimorphisms

\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\quad\textrm{given by}\quad\mathchar 28955_{\mathchar 29033}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891

\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\quad\textrm{given by}\quad\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}

and

\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12833\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\quad\textrm{given by}\quad\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28956_{\mathchar 29033}

are the pure braid group
\mathchar 29008_{\mathchar 29038}
, the pure twin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
and the pure triplet group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
, respectively. Pure braid groups are well-known to have complements of complex hyperplane arrangements as their Eilenberg–MacLane spaces. Pure twin groups and pure triplet groups are real counterparts of pure braid groups, and also admit suitable codimension two subspace arrangements as their Eilenberg–MacLane spaces
[
20
]
. Furthermore, these groups have topological interpretations similar to braid groups, and play the role of groups under the Alexander-Markov correspondence for some appropriate knot theories. Twin groups are related to doodles on the 2-sphere, which were introduced by Fenn and Taylor
[
11
]
as finite collections of simple closed curves on the 2-sphere without triple or higher intersections. Allowing self intersections of curves, Khovanov
[
18
]
extended the idea to finite collections of closed curves without triple or higher intersections on a closed oriented surface. Similarly, triplet groups are related to topological objects called noodles. Fixing a codimension one foliation (with singular points) on the 2-sphere, a noodle is a collection of closed curves on the 2-sphere such that no two intersection points belong to the same leaf of the foliation, there are no quadruple intersections and no intersection point can occupy a singular point of the foliation.

Khovanov
[
18
]
proved an analogue of Alexander Theorem by showing that every oriented doodle on a
\mathchar 28722
-sphere is closure of a twin. An analogue of Markov Theorem in this setting has been proved by Gotin
[
15
]
, though the idea has been implicit in
[
19
]
. The structure of the pure twin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
for small values of
\mathchar 29038
has been well-understood
[
6
,
13
,
24
]
, but understanding the general case seems challenging (see
[
23
]
for some progress). Concerning triplet groups, it has been shown in
[
26
]
that the pure triplet group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
is a non-abelian free group of finite rank for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
.

The virtual knot theory is a far reaching generalisation of the classical knot theory and was introduced by Kauffman in
[
16
]
. Motivated by the well-known Alexander-Markov correspondence for virtual knot theories, the preceding three extensions of
\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}
can be enlarged to their virtual avatars, namely, the virtual braid group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}

[
17
]
, the virtual twin group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}

[
6
,
27
]
and the virtual triplet group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
(which we introduce below).

There are natural epimorphisms

The kernels of these epimorphisms are the pure virtual braid group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29008_{\mathchar 29038}
, the pure virtual twin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
and the pure virtual triplet group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
, respectively. The virtual groups together with their pure subgroups make the following diagram commute.

The present paper is concerned with commutator subgroups, pure subgroups and certain geometrically motivated quotients of virtual braid groups, virtual twin groups and virtual triplet groups. It is known from
[
5
, Theorem 1.1]
that the commutator subgroup
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
of the virtual braid group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
is generated by
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
elements for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
, whereas
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28723}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is infinitely generated. It is still not known whether
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is finitely presented for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
. In this paper, we determine a precise finite presentation of the commutator subgroup
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
of
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}

(Theorem
2.2
) and the commutator subgroup
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
of
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
(Theorem
3.2
). A finite presentation of the pure virtual braid group is well-known due to Bardakov
[
2
]
. It has been proved in
[
25
]
that the pure virtual twin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
is an irreducible right-angled Artin group and a finite presentation has also been given for the same. We use the Reidemeister-Schreier method to derive a finite presentation of the pure virtual triplet group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
(Theorem
4.2
). This completes our understanding of these groups in the virtual setting. As by-products, we deduce that
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
and
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
are residually nilpotent if and only if
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
(Corollaries
2.4
and
3.4
).

Crystallographic groups play a crucial role in the study of groups of isometries of Euclidean spaces. In fact, there is a correspondence between torsion free crystallographic groups (called Bieberbach groups) and the class of compact flat Riemannian manifolds
[
9
, Theorem 2.1.1]
. Crystallographic quotients of virtual braid groups and virtual twin groups have been investigated in
[
28
]
. In this paper, we consider the case of virtual triplet groups. More precisely, we prove that
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is a crystallographic group of dimension
\mathchar 29038\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
(Theorem
5.4
), and that it contains infinitely many torsion elements (Corollary
5.6
). In fact, we give an explicit construction of infinitely many torsion elements in
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
.

We denote the
\mathchar 29038
-th term of the lower central series of a group
\mathchar 28999
by
\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785
, where
\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28999
and
\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28999\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28999^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is the commutator subgroup of
\mathchar 28999
.

2.
Presentation of commutator subgroup of virtual twin group

In this section, we give a finite presentation of the commutator subgroup
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
of
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
. This is achieved through a reduced presentation of
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
, which we prove first.

Theorem 2.1
.

The virtual twin group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}

\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
has the following reduced presentation:

(1)

\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}~\mathchar 12906~\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\delimiter 86414091
.

(2)

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724
,

has a presentation with generating set

and defining relations as follows:

It is known that the second and the third term of the lower central series coincide for the braid group
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
when
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723

[
14
]
and coincide for the virtual braid group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}
when
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28724

[
3
, Proposition 7(c)]
. The same assertion does not hold for twin groups
[
30
, Theorem 4.5]
. However, it turns out that the assertion holds for virtual twin groups.

Proposition 2.3
.

\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
.

Proof.

It is enough to prove that
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is abelian. The group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
is generated by

\tilde{\mathchar 29043}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tilde{\mathchar 28954}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891

where
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
. It is easy to see that

∎

Corollary 2.4
.

\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
is residually nilpotent if and only if
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
.

We conclude the section with a result on freeness of commutator subgroup of
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
. A graph is called
chordal
if each of its cycles with more than three vertices has a chord (an edge joining two vertices that are not adjacent in the cycle). A
clique
(or a complete subgraph) of a graph is a subset
\mathchar 28995
of vertices such that every two vertices in
\mathchar 28995
are connected by an edge. It is well-known that a graph is chordal if and only if its vertices can be ordered in such a way that the lesser neighbours of each vertex form a clique.

Proposition 2.5
.

The commutator subgroup of
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
is free if and only if
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28724
.

Proof.

By
[
25
, Theorem 3.3]
, the pure virtual twin group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}
is an irreducible right-angled Artin group for each
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, and has a presentation

\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 12349\big\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891~\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038~\mathchar 12906~\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\text{ for distinct integers }\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29036\big\delimiter 86414091\mathchar 24891

where
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}
for each
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
and
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\dots\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\dots\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}

for each
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
and
\mathchar 29034\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
. The assertion is now immediate for
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723
.

The graph of
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28724}
(see Figure
1
) is vacuously chordal.

Figure 1
.
The graph of
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28724}
.

Figure 2
.
The graph of
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28725}
.

By
[
29
, Corollary 4.4]
, the commutator subgroup of a right-angled Artin group is free if and only if its associated graph is chordal, and hence
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28724}
is free.

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28725
, fix an ordering on the vertex set of the graph, for example, it could be the lexicographic ordering in our case. Let
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
be a maximal vertex and
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29041\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}\mathchar 8814\{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\}
be three distinct integers. Then both
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29041}
and
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29042}
are lesser neighbours of
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
, but there cannot be an edge between
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29041}
and
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29042}
. Thus, lesser neighbours of the vertex
\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}
do not form a clique, and hence the graph is not chordal.
∎

Proposition 3.3
.

\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785

Proof.

Consider the short exact sequence

\mathchar 28721\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314

Since coset of each
\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}
maps to the generator of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}_{\mathchar 28722}
, it follows that there exist

for each

, where
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
. Using the braid relation in
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785
, we obtain

(3.0.19)

\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 28721}\mod\mathchar 28941_{\mathchar 28723}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785

Theorem 4.2
.

The pure virtual triplet group
\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
has a presentation with generating set
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 12349\left\{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\right\}
and defining relations

\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29034\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 24891

where
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12604\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 314

Proof.

We begin by observing that

\overline{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939\quad\textrm{and}\quad\overline{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}}\ldots\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29035}}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}}\ldots\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29035}}

for words
\mathchar 28939
and
\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 29034}
in the generators
\left\{\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\right\}
. Thus, we have

\mathchar 28941\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721

and

\mathchar 28941\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28950\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28950^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28950\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28950^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}

for each
\mathchar 28950\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29005}_{\mathchar 29038}
and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
. It follows from Lemma
4.1
that each
\mathchar 28941\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28950\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29049_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785
lie in
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 8820\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
. Conversely, if

5.
Crystallographic quotients of virtual triplet groups

A closed subgroup
\mathchar 29000
of a Hausdorff topological group
\mathchar 28999
is said to be
uniform
if
\mathchar 28999\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29000
is a compact space. A discrete and uniform subgroup
\mathchar 28999
of
~\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}^{\mathchar 29038}\rtimes\operatorname{\mathchar 29007}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 24891\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785
is called a
crystallographic group
of dimension
\mathchar 29038
. If in addition
\mathchar 28999
is torsion free, then it is called a
Bieberbach group
of dimension
\mathchar 29038
. The following characterisation of crystallographic groups is well-known
[
12
, Lemma 8]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

A group
\mathchar 28999
is a crystallographic group if and only if there is an integer
\mathchar 29038
, a finite group
\mathchar 29000
and a short exact sequence

\mathchar 28720\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28999\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathchar 28945}}{{\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}}}\mathchar 29000\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28721

such that the integral representation
\mathchar 28674\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29000\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\operatorname{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29044}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785
defined by
\mathchar 28674\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29050\mathchar 29048\mathchar 29050^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
is faithful, where
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29000
,
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038}
and
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28999
is such that
\mathchar 28945\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29032
.

The group
\mathchar 29000
is called the
holonomy group
of
\mathchar 28999
, the integer
\mathchar 29038
is called the
dimension
of
\mathchar 28999
, and
\mathchar 28674\mathchar 12346\mathchar 29000\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\operatorname{\mathchar 28993\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29044}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038}\right\delimiter 84054785
is called the
holonomy representation
of
\mathchar 28999
. It is known that any
finite group is the holonomy group of some flat manifold
[
8
, Theorem III.5.2]
. Furthermore, there is a correspondence between the class of Bieberbach groups and the class of compact flat Riemannian manifolds
[
9
, Theorem 2.1.1]
. Crystallographic quotients of virtual braid and virtual twin groups have been considered in
[
28
, Theorem 2.4]
and
[
28
, Theorem 3.5]
, respectively.

Theorem 5.2
.

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, there is a split short exact sequence

\mathchar 28721\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29008_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28721

such that the group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29008_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is a crystallographic group of dimension
\mathchar 29038\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785
.

Theorem 5.3
.

For
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, there is a split short exact sequence

\mathchar 28721\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29011_{\mathchar 29038}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 28721

such that the group
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}

\mathchar 29038\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28722

We prove the following result for virtual triplet groups.

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29042}
be positive integers (not necessarily distinct) each greater than 1 such that
\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
. Then
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
admits infinitely many elements of order
\operatorname{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29027\mathchar 29037}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29042}\right\delimiter 84054785
. Further, there exists such an element whose corresponding permutation has cycle type
\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\ldots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29042}\right\delimiter 84054785
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.5
, there exist elements
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}
whose cosets have order
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29035}
in
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
for each
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29042
, where

\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\begin{cases}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 28721}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722}\right\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}}\delimiter 84054785~\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 28721}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785&\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\\
\\
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4945\displaylimits_{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29007}_{\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29035}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29042_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29035}}\right\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}}\delimiter 84054785~\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785&\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathchar 28722\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29042\mathchar 24891\end{cases}

\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
,
\mathchar 29042_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29043_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8235
2 and
\mathchar 28953\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29035}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29035}
. Since
\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721}\mathinner{\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}\mathpunct{\mathchar 513}}\mathchar 28954_{\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29035_{\mathchar 29033}}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29036}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
and

\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}

.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

The preceding corollary shows that
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
is not a Bieberbach group. In the spirit of
[
4
, Question 3.2]
, it is interesting to determine Bieberbach subgroups of
\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29008\mathchar 29014\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29038}^{{}^{\mathchar 560}}
.
